[codex]   explicitly.

[codex] ## Architecture and Security Rules
[codex] 
[codex] - Keep controllers thin and delegate application behavior to services.
[codex]   Controllers must never import repositories.
[codex] - Keep consumers thin and delegate to services. Consumers must never import
[codex]   repositories.
[codex] - Keep transaction boundaries in the service layer and persistence behind
[codex]   repositories.
[codex] - Use Jakarta Persistence APIs only. Never import `org.hibernate.*` or depend on
[codex]   Hibernate-specific behavior.
[codex] - Extend the shared auditable or soft-deletable base entity that matches the
[codex]   domain lifecycle; consult the shared Spring Boot conventions before creating
[codex]   an entity.
[codex] - Access external exchange-rate systems through `ExchangeRateProvider`. The
[codex]   service layer must never reference FRED-specific clients or types.
[codex] - Publish domain events from services. Do not inject message publishers into
[codex]   services; bridge domain events to external messages in listeners.
[codex] - Apply `@SchedulerLock` to scheduled work that must execute once across pods.
[codex] - Cache read paths only where the documented cache strategy calls for it, and
[codex]   evict affected cache entries after imports or mutations.
[codex] - Preserve claims-header-based authorization and enforce endpoint permissions
[codex]   with `@PreAuthorize`. Do not trust client-supplied identity or permission
[codex]   headers outside the platform's validated gateway path.
[codex] - Treat role definitions and atomic permission ownership as permission-service
[codex]   concerns. Do not redefine the ecosystem RBAC model in this repository.
[codex]

[codex] # Service-Common Artifact Resolution
[codex] 
[codex] This document explains the split between local `mavenLocal()` resolution and
[codex] the GitHub Packages path used by GitHub Actions and release builds.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Version Scope
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` is a shared Java library version, not the deployment version
[codex] for the whole Budget Analyzer stack. Bump and release it when
[codex] `spring-platform`, `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, or `service-web`
[codex] changes. Do not change it only because a service image, frontend artifact,
[codex] route, production overlay, `/api-docs` metadata file, or OCI deployment
[codex] configuration changed.
[codex] 
[codex] Java services can release runtime artifacts while continuing to consume the
[codex] same checked-in `serviceCommon` coordinate from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`.
[codex] The production deployment identity is the digest-pinned image inventory and
[codex] deployment manifest, which may contain mixed service artifact versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Local Contributor Flow
[codex] 
[codex] The supported side-by-side workspace flow must stay credential-free.
[codex] 
[codex] If you follow [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) and run local
[codex] development through orchestration and Tilt:
[codex] 
[codex] - clone the repos side by side
[codex] - open the sibling `orchestration/` repo
[codex] - run `./setup.sh`
[codex] - run `tilt up`
[codex] 
[codex] you should not need `GITHUB_ACTOR`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a personal access token
[codex] just to start the app locally.
[codex] 
[codex] That works because the local build path stays local-first:
[codex] 
[codex] - Java service repos keep `mavenLocal()` first
[codex] - Tilt runs the `service-common-publish` resource first
[codex] - `service-common-publish` runs `publishToMavenLocal`
[codex] - downstream host-side Gradle builds then resolve `service-common` from Maven
[codex]   Local
[codex] 
[codex] If you hit a local `service-common` resolution error while working in the full
[codex] workspace, republish it locally and rerun the service build:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../service-common
[codex] ./gradlew clean build publishToMavenLocal
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## When GitHub Packages Credentials Are Required
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub Packages credentials are a CI/release concern, not a getting-started
[codex] prerequisite.
[codex] 
[codex] You need GitHub Packages credentials when all of these are true:
[codex] 
[codex] - the build is not using the local orchestration/Tilt path
[codex] - `service-common` is not already available through Maven Local
[codex] - the build must resolve the published `org.budgetanalyzer` artifacts remotely
[codex]   from `https://maven.pkg.github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common`
[codex] 
[codex] Typical examples:
[codex] 
[codex] - default GitHub Actions `build.yml` runs in `transaction-service`,
[codex]   `currency-service`, `permission-service`, and `session-gateway`
[codex] - release-image Docker builds
[codex] - isolated CI or clean-builder image builds
[codex] - manual clean-shell verification of remote published artifacts
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` itself now refreshes the current checked-in snapshot from
[codex] `main` through its `publish-snapshot.yml` workflow. That remote snapshot path
[codex] exists to support isolated CI/release builds; it does not change the local
[codex] contributor contract.
[codex] 
[codex] For the Java consumer repos, the default build workflow contract is now:
[codex] 
[codex] - read the pinned `serviceCommon` version from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
[codex] - fail fast if `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` or
[codex]   `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN` is missing
[codex] - preflight the versioned `service-core` and `service-web` POM URLs in GitHub
[codex]   Packages
[codex] - run `./gradlew build` with `GITHUB_ACTOR` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported from
[codex]   that same secret pair
[codex] 
[codex] ## GitHub Actions Environment
[codex] 
[codex] When a workflow intentionally resolves `service-common` remotely from GitHub
[codex] Packages, Gradle needs both a token and a package username.
[codex] 
[codex] For cross-repo private Maven/Gradle consumption, do not assume the workflow
[codex] repo's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` can read `service-common`. GitHub still treats these
[codex] packages as repository-scoped, so the consuming workflow needs an explicit
[codex] GitHub Packages credential.
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the current repo wiring expects:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] `GITHUB_ACTOR` here means the username that owns the GitHub Packages credential,
[codex] not necessarily `${{ github.actor }}`. `GITHUB_TOKEN` here means the secret used
[codex] for remote package reads, not necessarily the workflow repo's default token.
[codex] This requirement applies to the remote GitHub Packages path used by default
[codex] `build.yml` and release workflows. It does not apply to the normal `tilt up`
[codex] contributor flow.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Containerized Release Builds
[codex] 
[codex] The Java service Dockerfiles now support remote `service-common` resolution for
[codex] release and isolated CI builds without copying host `.m2` state into the build
[codex] context and without checking out sibling source trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Pass the workflow credentials into BuildKit as secrets:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] docker build \
[codex]   --secret id=github_actor,env=GITHUB_ACTOR \
[codex]   --secret id=github_token,env=GITHUB_TOKEN \
[codex]   -f Dockerfile .
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the release job should still expose those same credential
[codex] values under the env names the current Gradle builds read:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The Dockerfiles read those values only inside the Gradle builder-stage `RUN`
[codex] steps, so the credentials are not written into the image, filesystem layers, or
[codex] checked-in repo files.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Why Public Package Visibility Does Not Remove This
[codex] 
[codex] Making the `service-common` Maven packages public in the GitHub UI does not
[codex] remove the Maven/Gradle authentication requirement.
[codex] 
[codex] Keep the release workflow package-visibility and workflow-access cleanup, but do not
[codex] treat it as the fix for local contributor UX. The fix for local contributor UX
[codex] is keeping the side-by-side workspace and Tilt flow local-first and
[codex] credential-free.
[codex]

[codex] exec
[codex] /bin/bash -lc "sed -n '1,260p' renovate-presets/default.json" in /workspace/orchestration

[codex] ## Phase 4: Onboard currency-service
[codex] 
[codex] ### Workspace
[codex] 
[codex] ../currency-service
[codex] 
[codex] ### Goal
[codex] 
[codex] Provide dependency update proposals and resolved vulnerability alerts for this service.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Scope
[codex] 
[codex] Repository Renovate config, dependency-submission workflow and nearest docs.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Non-goals
[codex] 
[codex] No dependency upgrades, service logic changes, releases or sibling writes.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Required context
[codex] 
[codex] Read local `AGENTS.md`, build/catalog/workflows, the shared preset, and
[codex] `../orchestration/docs/development/service-common-artifact-resolution.md`.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Execution steps
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Add the thin shared-preset config and verify Gradle, wrapper, image and Actions
[codex]    discovery, including the declared `serviceCommon` dependency.
[codex] 2. Apply the official dependency-submission pattern from Phase 3 with basic caching
[codex]    and this repo's existing GitHub Packages read credentials. Keep package-read
[codex]    credentials distinct from the GitHub token used for graph submission.
[codex] 3. Cover resolved application/runtime/test dependencies and document the trusted
[codex]    branch schedule and bot PR validation behavior.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Implementation notes
[codex] 
[codex] An existing successful remote-package build can establish credential availability.
[codex] Do not mask a failed resolution with local cached artifacts or omit the dependency.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Validation
[codex] 
[codex] Run the config validator, extraction/lookup and actionlint; inspect a generated
[codex] dependency snapshot for this service's transitive runtime tree. Record unavailable
[codex] authenticated validation as pending, never as passed.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Completion criteria
[codex] 
[codex] Validated repo-local configuration and a complete snapshot, or an explicit missing
[codex] credential prerequisite that must be resolved before marking this phase complete.
[codex]

[codex] # Dependency Automation
[codex] 
[codex] The workspace-wide operating policy, activation steps, cost boundary, and
[codex] failure triage are owned by
[codex] [orchestration's dependency automation guide](../../orchestration/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] This document records only the `service-common` integration and review checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Update discovery
[codex] 
[codex] `renovate.json` extends the shared Budget Analyzer preset. Renovate's native
[codex] Gradle, Gradle Wrapper, and GitHub Actions managers discover this repository's
[codex] version catalog, build scripts, wrapper distribution, and workflow actions.
[codex] There are currently no Dockerfiles in this repository. Add file-specific
[codex] manager configuration only if a future dependency declaration is not covered
[codex] by a native manager.
[codex] 
[codex] The Phase 3 local extraction found 52 dependency occurrences across 12 files:
[codex] 33 Gradle occurrences in seven files, one wrapper occurrence, and 18 GitHub
[codex] Actions occurrences in four workflows. Maven and Gradle lookups produced 41
[codex] candidate branches before scheduling, concurrency limits, and dashboard
[codex] approval. Maintained-line and later-major proposals were both visible for the
[codex] Spring platform, documentation, test, locking, and wrapper dependencies. The
[codex] Actions references were extracted, but authenticated GitHub lookups remain a
[codex] post-publication hosted check because no GitHub token is supplied to local
[codex] validation.
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate proposes changes only to direct declarations. It does not turn every
[codex] version inherited from the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, or Spring Modulith BOMs
[codex] into an independently updateable declaration. The resolved dependency graph
[codex] described below is the security inventory for those inherited versions.
[codex] 
[codex] Major updates remain visible in the Dependency Dashboard and require approval.
[codex] In particular, Spring Boot 4 and the related Spring Cloud, Spring Framework,
[codex] Spring Security, Spring Modulith, SpringDoc, Testcontainers, ShedLock, and test
[codex] platform lines are a compatibility migration, not a routine patch. Renovate
[codex] cannot select the correct release-train combination or coordinate consumer
[codex] services by itself.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Resolved dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] `basic` cache provider. Its job token receives only `contents: write`, which is
[codex] required by GitHub's Dependency Submission API. The graph is submitted
[codex] directly and is not retained as a workflow artifact or published as a Build
[codex] Scan.
[codex] 
[codex] The action's default resolution task visits all projects and all resolvable
[codex] configurations. Do not add project or configuration filters without proving
[codex] equivalent coverage: the platform modules contribute imported BOMs, while the
[codex] library modules contribute compile, runtime, and test dependency trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Local Phase 3 validation generated an official plugin snapshot without
[codex] submitting it. The snapshot contained 216 resolved coordinate/version entries
[codex] across the four subprojects. It included the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, and
[codex] Spring Modulith BOM coordinates and these inherited component families where
[codex] the current build actually resolves them:
[codex] 
[codex] | Component family | Resolution evidence |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Spring Framework | Library runtime and test classpaths |
[codex] | Spring Security | `service-core` and `service-web` test classpaths; compile-only APIs are also resolved through compile classpaths |
[codex] | Embedded Tomcat | `service-web` test runtime, which exercises the servlet stack |
[codex] | Netty | `service-web` test runtime, which exercises the reactive stack |
[codex] | Jackson | `service-core` and `service-web` runtime and test classpaths |
[codex] 
[codex] The entry count and resolved versions will change as the checked-in dependency
[codex] selection changes; they are evidence from onboarding, not a desired-version
[codex] inventory. A successful local snapshot proves generation and transitive
[codex] coverage, but only a hosted run after repository activation proves submission
[codex] and creates Dependabot alert input.
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub's dependency graph reports resolved packages but does not decide which
[codex] BOM or override should remediate an inherited finding. A Dependabot alert is
[codex] also not proof that an available Spring Boot update fixes every Framework,
[codex] Security, Jackson, Tomcat, or Netty advisory. Trace the affected configuration
[codex] with Gradle dependency insight and review any override as a service-owner
[codex] decision.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Bot pull request checks
[codex] 
[codex] For every Renovate pull request:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Keep the shared preset's no-automerge and dashboard-approval behavior.
[codex] 2. Review the resolved dependency diff, release notes, Java 25 and Spring
[codex]    release-train compatibility, and whether an inherited advisory is actually
[codex]    remediated.
[codex] 3. Run the repository-required validation in order:
[codex] 
[codex]    ```bash
[codex]    ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex]    ./gradlew clean build
[codex]    ```
[codex] 
[codex] 4. For platform or shared-library changes, validate all consuming services
[codex]    before release as required by
[codex]    [versioning and compatibility](versioning-and-compatibility.md). Do not
[codex]    publish artifacts or update consumers from an unreviewed bot branch.
[codex] 
[codex] Graph generation failures are failures, not clean security results. Preserve
[codex] dependency-resolution and submission errors for triage; do not substitute
[codex] Maven Local, omit configurations, or enable a hosted Build Scan to make the job
[codex] pass.
[codex] 230:[docs/dependency-automation.md](docs/dependency-automation.md). Dependency graph
[codex]

[codex]         type: string

[codex]         description: "Optional Docker tag label for an ad hoc manual image; never use latest"
[codex]         required: false
[codex]         type: string
[codex]

[codex]       contents: read

[codex]         with:

[codex]           fetch-depth: 0
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Resolve release inputs
[codex]         id: release
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         env:
[codex]           REQUESTED_RELEASE_REF: ${{ github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && github.event.inputs.release_ref || github.ref_name }}
[codex]           REQUESTED_DOCKER_LABEL: ${{ github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && github.event.inputs.docker_label || '' }}
[codex]           SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]           SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           event_name="${GITHUB_EVENT_NAME}"
[codex]           release_ref="${REQUESTED_RELEASE_REF}"
[codex]           requested_docker_label="${REQUESTED_DOCKER_LABEL}"
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ -z "${release_ref}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "release_ref is required." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${release_ref}" =~ ^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$ ]]; then
[codex]             default_docker_label="${release_ref#v}"
[codex]           else
[codex]             default_docker_label=""
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${event_name}" == "push" ]]; then
[codex]             if [[ -z "${default_docker_label}" ]]; then
[codex]               echo "Release tag pushes must use SemVer tags like v1.2.3, got ${release_ref}." >&2
[codex]               exit 1
[codex]             fi
[codex]             if [[ -n "${requested_docker_label}" ]]; then
[codex]               echo "docker_label is only supported for workflow_dispatch." >&2
[codex]               exit 1
[codex]             fi
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${event_name}" == "workflow_dispatch" && -z "${default_docker_label}" && -z "${requested_docker_label}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "workflow_dispatch release_ref is not a SemVer release tag, so docker_label is required." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           docker_label="${requested_docker_label:-${default_docker_label}}"
[codex]           if [[ ! "${docker_label}" =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,127}$ || "${docker_label}" == "latest" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "Resolved docker_label is not Docker-safe or is disallowed: ${docker_label}." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ -z "${SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME}" || -z "${SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "Missing SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME or SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           echo "docker_label=${docker_label}" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Resolve checked-out revision
[codex]         id: source
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           commit_sha="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
[codex]           echo "commit_sha=${commit_sha}" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex]

[codex]         with:

[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Set up Docker Buildx

[codex] # Currency Service
[codex] 
[codex] > "Archetype: service. Role: Manages currencies and exchange rates with external provider integration."
[codex] >
[codex] > — [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#tree-position)
[codex] 
[codex] [![Build](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/currency-service/actions/workflows/build.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/currency-service/actions/workflows/build.yml)
[codex] 
[codex] Spring Boot microservice that automates exchange rate imports from the [Federal Reserve Economic Data (FRED)](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/) API, delivering reliable currency and exchange rate data for Budget Analyzer.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Core Capabilities
[codex] 
[codex] - **Automated FRED imports** - Daily scheduled imports with provider abstraction ([details](docs/fred-integration.md))
[codex] - **Currency series management** - CRUD operations for exchange rate time series ([API docs](docs/api/README.md))
[codex] - **Historical rate queries** - Date range filtering with Redis caching (80-95% hit rate)
[codex] - **Distributed coordination** - Multi-pod safe scheduling with ShedLock
[codex] - **Event-driven architecture** - Guaranteed delivery via transactional outbox + RabbitMQ
[codex] 
[codex] ## Quick Start
[codex] 
[codex] **Prerequisites:** JDK 25, Docker/Docker Compose
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # 1. Start infrastructure
[codex] cd ../orchestration && tilt up
[codex] 
[codex] # 2. Export credentials and start the service
[codex] cd ../currency-service
[codex] export FRED_API_KEY=your_api_key_here
[codex] export SPRING_DATASOURCE_PASSWORD=your_db_password
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_PASSWORD=your_rabbitmq_password
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PASSWORD=your_redis_password
[codex] # See docs/local-development.md for full env var list (TLS, ports, etc.)
[codex] ./gradlew bootRun
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Dev server:** `http://localhost:8084` | **Swagger UI:** `http://localhost:8084/swagger-ui.html`
[codex] 
[codex] ## Technology Stack
[codex] 
[codex] Java 25, Spring Boot 3.x (Web, Data JPA, Modulith, Actuator), PostgreSQL, Redis, RabbitMQ, ShedLock, Flyway, SpringDoc OpenAPI, TestContainers + JUnit 5
[codex] 
[codex] ## Build and Test
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport
[codex] ```

[codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html` and
[codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/jacocoTestReport.xml`. `check` enforces the
[codex] configured coverage gates.

[codex] # Local Development
[codex] 
[codex] ## Prerequisites
[codex] 
[codex] - JDK 25
[codex] - Docker and Docker Compose (for infrastructure)
[codex] 
[codex] **Ecosystem setup guides:**
[codex] - [getting-started.md](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/development/getting-started.md)
[codex] - [database-setup.md](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/development/database-setup.md)
[codex] - [service-common artifact resolution](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/development/service-common-artifact-resolution.md)
[codex] 
[codex] > **Note:** This service uses the `currency` database (not `budget_analyzer`). Local builds resolve `service-common` from `mavenLocal()` — no GitHub credentials required.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Running Locally
[codex] 
[codex] Start shared infrastructure from the orchestration repo:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../orchestration
[codex] tilt up
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] In another terminal, export the required environment variables and start the service:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../currency-service
[codex] 
[codex] export FRED_API_KEY=your_api_key_here
[codex] export SPRING_DATASOURCE_PASSWORD=your_currency_database_password
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_PASSWORD=your_currency_service_rabbitmq_password
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_HOST=localhost
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_PORT=5671
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_SSL_ENABLED=true
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_SSL_BUNDLE=infra-ca
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PASSWORD=your_currency_service_redis_password
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST="${SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST:-localhost}"
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PORT="${SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PORT:-6379}"
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_SSL_ENABLED=true
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_SSL_BUNDLE=infra-ca
[codex] export INFRA_CA_CERT_PATH="file:$(cd ../orchestration && pwd)/nginx/certs/infra/infra-ca.pem"
[codex] 
[codex] ./gradlew bootRun
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] If you are reusing values from `../orchestration/.env`, map
[codex] `POSTGRES_CURRENCY_SERVICE_PASSWORD`,
[codex] `RABBITMQ_CURRENCY_SERVICE_PASSWORD`, and
[codex] `REDIS_CURRENCY_SERVICE_PASSWORD` into the Spring environment variables above.
[codex] 
[codex] ### TLS Notes
[codex] 
[codex] - The CA path must point at the host-side file created by `../orchestration/scripts/bootstrap/setup-infra-tls.sh`.
[codex] - RabbitMQ hostname verification is enabled. `SPRING_RABBITMQ_HOST` must use a name covered by the broker certificate SANs.
[codex] - The default host-side certificate includes `localhost`, `rabbitmq.infrastructure`, `rabbitmq.infrastructure.svc`, and `rabbitmq.infrastructure.svc.cluster.local`. Using a different hostname will fail the TLS handshake until the certificate is regenerated with that SAN.
[codex]

[codex] ## Building
[codex] 
[codex] Dependency versions for shared Spring libraries are managed by the service-common
[codex] `spring-cloud-platform` artifact. Local builds resolve that platform, `service-web`, and
[codex] `service-core` from `mavenLocal()` first; after changing service-common platform metadata, publish it
[codex] locally before rebuilding this service.
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] ./gradlew clean build       # Clean and build
[codex] ./gradlew test              # Run tests
[codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport  # Generate JaCoCo coverage reports
[codex] ./gradlew spotlessCheck     # Check code style
[codex] ./gradlew clean spotlessApply  # Apply code formatting
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] JaCoCo writes the HTML report to `build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html`
[codex] and the XML report to `build/reports/jacoco/test/jacocoTestReport.xml`.
[codex] `check` enforces the Phase 2 coverage gates: 90% line coverage and 85% branch
[codex] coverage. The recorded baseline is 96.43% line / 88.71% branch; ratchet toward
[codex] critical utility and provider path coverage.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Testing
[codex] 
[codex] Before writing or changing tests, consult the
[codex] [canonical service-common testing patterns](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common/blob/main/docs/testing-patterns.md).
[codex] Keep application-owned Spring beans real. Integration tests use Testcontainers for PostgreSQL,
[codex] Redis, and RabbitMQ, and use WireMock only for the external FRED HTTP boundary.
[codex] 
[codex] Docker must be running, but the integration suite does not require shared local infrastructure or a
[codex] real FRED API key. `AbstractIntegrationTest` supplies the containers, and tests that call FRED extend
[codex] `AbstractWireMockTest` and register controlled FRED responses through `FredApiStubs`.
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Run one integration suite
[codex] ./gradlew test --tests '*EventListenerIntegrationTest'
[codex] 
[codex] # Run all messaging integration suites
[codex] ./gradlew test --tests '*EventListenerIntegrationTest' \
[codex]   --tests '*MessageConsumerIntegrationTest' \
[codex]   --tests '*EndToEndMessagingFlowIntegrationTest'
[codex] ```

[codex] 333-[codex] ## Selected tools and cost
[codex] 334-[codex] 
[codex] 335-[codex] | Purpose | Selection | Cost and boundary |
[codex] 336-[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] 337-[codex] | Version and digest update PRs | Open-source Renovate, run by the Mend Renovate Community GitHub App | The app explicitly supports public and private repositories without a paid plan. No self-hosted bot infrastructure is needed. |
[codex] 338:[codex] | Dependency vulnerability alerts | GitHub dependency graph and Dependabot alerts | Available on GitHub Free. GitHub's hosted service is proprietary; the update engine and added scanning tools are open source. |
[codex] 339:[codex] | Resolved Java dependency graph | Official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` | Use the open-source `cache-provider: basic` path, not the proprietary enhanced cache or a preview subscription. |
[codex] 340-[codex] | Image packages and vulnerabilities | Trivy CLI/action | Open source; no Aqua subscription, server, or commercial database required. |
[codex] 341-[codex] | Frontend and Go verification | `npm audit` and `govulncheck` | Existing ecosystem tools; no subscription. |
[codex] 342-[codex] 
[codex] 343-[codex] Renovate is the sole update-PR owner. Do not add Dependabot version-update
[codex] 344-[codex] configuration or enable overlapping Dependabot security-update PRs. Renovate can
[codex] --
[codex] 376-[codex] Tilt strings, and download URLs, use Renovate's documented declarative regex
[codex] 377-[codex] manager and annotation comments. This is configuration of an existing tool, not a
[codex] 378-[codex] new dependency crawler. Do not build custom scanners, advisory matching code,
[codex] 379-[codex] version-selection scripts, lifecycle services, dashboards, or cross-repo bots.
[codex] 380-[codex] 
[codex] 381:[codex] Put common rules in `renovate-presets/default.json` in orchestration. Sibling
[codex] 382:[codex] `renovate.json` files extend
[codex] 383:[codex] `github>budgetanalyzer/orchestration//renovate-presets/default`; keep file-specific
[codex] 384-[codex] extraction rules in their owning repository. Publish the preset before activating
[codex] 385-[codex] consumers. Documentation belongs in a future `docs/dependency-automation.md`,
[codex] 386-[codex] with sibling docs linking to it instead of copying detailed policy.
[codex] 387-[codex] 
[codex] 388-[codex] Initial policy:
[codex] --
[codex] 469-[codex] 
[codex] 470-[codex] Implementation phases prepare reviewable local files. No phase authorizes git
[codex] 471-[codex] writes, merging dependency PRs, releases, certificate writes, or cluster changes.
[codex] 472-[codex] The user owns committing and publishing changes. Before final observation, a
[codex] 473-[codex] repository administrator installs the free Renovate App only on the scoped repos,
[codex] 474:[codex] enables dependency graph and Dependabot alerts, grants the app alert-read access,
[codex] 475-[codex] and confirms duplicate update-PR services are off. Prepare all configuration and
[codex] 476-[codex] documentation before this activation handoff. Do not change GitHub settings while

[codex] 1528-[codex] 
[codex] 1529-[codex] ### Workspace
[codex] 1530-[codex] # Service-Common Artifact Resolution
[codex] 1531-[codex] 
[codex] 1532:[codex] This document explains the split between local `mavenLocal()` resolution and
[codex] 1533-[codex] the GitHub Packages path used by GitHub Actions and release builds.
[codex] 1534-[codex] 
[codex] 1535-[codex] ## Version Scope
[codex] 1536-[codex] 
[codex] 1537-[codex] `service-common` is a shared Java library version, not the deployment version
[codex] --
[codex] 1556-[codex] - clone the repos side by side
[codex] 1557-[codex] - open the sibling `orchestration/` repo
[codex] 1558-[codex] - run `./setup.sh`
[codex] 1559-[codex] - run `tilt up`
[codex] 1560-[codex] 
[codex] 1561:[codex] you should not need `GITHUB_ACTOR`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a personal access token
[codex] 1562-[codex] just to start the app locally.
[codex] 1563-[codex] 
[codex] 1564-[codex] That works because the local build path stays local-first:
[codex] 1565-[codex] 
[codex] 1566:[codex] - Java service repos keep `mavenLocal()` first
[codex] 1567-[codex] - Tilt runs the `service-common-publish` resource first
[codex] 1568-[codex] - `service-common-publish` runs `publishToMavenLocal`
[codex] 1569-[codex] - downstream host-side Gradle builds then resolve `service-common` from Maven
[codex] 1570-[codex]   Local
[codex] 1571-[codex] 
[codex] --
[codex] 1595-[codex]   `currency-service`, `permission-service`, and `session-gateway`
[codex] 1596-[codex] - release-image Docker builds
[codex] 1597-[codex] - isolated CI or clean-builder image builds
[codex] 1598-[codex] - manual clean-shell verification of remote published artifacts
[codex] 1599-[codex] 
[codex] 1600:[codex] `service-common` itself now refreshes the current checked-in snapshot from
[codex] 1601:[codex] `main` through its `publish-snapshot.yml` workflow. That remote snapshot path
[codex] 1602-[codex] exists to support isolated CI/release builds; it does not change the local
[codex] 1603-[codex] contributor contract.
[codex] 1604-[codex] 
[codex] 1605-[codex] For the Java consumer repos, the default build workflow contract is now:
[codex] 1606-[codex] 
[codex] 1607-[codex] - read the pinned `serviceCommon` version from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
[codex] 1608-[codex] - fail fast if `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` or
[codex] 1609-[codex]   `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN` is missing
[codex] 1610-[codex] - preflight the versioned `service-core` and `service-web` POM URLs in GitHub
[codex] 1611-[codex]   Packages

[codex] 1878-[codex] ## Ownership and policy
[codex] 1879-[codex] 
[codex] 1880-[codex] Renovate is the only service allowed to open dependency update pull requests.
[codex] 1881:[codex] Dependabot remains alert-only: keep the dependency graph and Dependabot alerts
[codex] 1882-[codex] enabled, disable Dependabot version updates and overlapping Dependabot security
[codex] 1883-[codex] update pull requests, and let Renovate consume alerts when its App permission
[codex] 1884-[codex] allows that. Never enable automerge, including for security fixes.
[codex] 1885-[codex] 
[codex] 1886-[codex] The shared policy is
[codex] 1887:[codex] [`renovate-presets/default.json`](../renovate-presets/default.json). Repository
[codex] 1888-[codex] configuration extends it and adds only repository-specific manager paths and
[codex] 1889-[codex] extraction rules. Routine pull requests are created weekly, with no more than
[codex] 1890-[codex] three open routine pull requests and two new routine pull requests per hour in
[codex] 1891-[codex] one repository. Vulnerability fixes use Renovate's dedicated alert path and an
[codex] 1892-[codex] unrestricted schedule. Major, chart, stateful, platform, and checksum-coupled
[codex] --
[codex] 1915-[codex] ## Cost and service boundary
[codex] 1916-[codex] 
[codex] 1917-[codex] Use the open-source Renovate engine through the free Mend Renovate Community
[codex] 1918-[codex] GitHub App. Do not provision a self-hosted bot or opt into paid Merge Confidence,
[codex] 1919-[codex] enterprise scheduling, enhanced cache, a trial, or another subscription. The
[codex] 1920:[codex] selected security evidence uses GitHub's dependency graph and Dependabot alerts,
[codex] 1921:[codex] Gradle's open-source basic dependency-submission path, Trivy, `npm audit`, and
[codex] 1922-[codex] `govulncheck`.
[codex] 1923-[codex] 
[codex] 1924-[codex] GitHub Actions usage is separate from Mend hosting. Public repositories can use
[codex] 1925-[codex] standard hosted Linux runners. Before enabling automation for a private
[codex] 1926-[codex] repository, confirm its included Actions allowance and that paid overages are
[codex] --
[codex] 1944-[codex] 3. Confirm repository visibility, Actions billing/overage settings, and that the
[codex] 1945-[codex]    free Mend service supports every scoped repository and required authenticated
[codex] 1946-[codex]    Maven lookup within its job limits.
[codex] 1947-[codex] 4. Install the Renovate Community App only on the repositories listed in the
[codex] 1948-[codex]    rollout plan. Grant read access to Dependabot alerts when available.
[codex] 1949:[codex] 5. Enable the dependency graph and Dependabot alerts. Disable Dependabot version
[codex] 1950-[codex]    updates and automatic Dependabot security-update pull requests.
[codex] 1951-[codex] 6. Configure private Maven access only through Mend-supported encrypted secrets
[codex] 1952-[codex]    and host rules. Never commit a token and never use `pull_request_target` to
[codex] 1953-[codex]    expose trusted credentials to dependency branches.
[codex] 1954-[codex] 7. Run onboarding for orchestration first. Inspect the resolved configuration,
[codex] --

[codex] 2247-[codex] # Service-Common Artifact Resolution
[codex] 2248-[codex] 
[codex] 2249:[codex] This document explains the split between local `mavenLocal()` resolution and
[codex] 2250-[codex] the GitHub Packages path used by GitHub Actions and release builds.
[codex] 2251-[codex] 
[codex] 2252-[codex] ## Version Scope
[codex] 2253-[codex] 
[codex] 2254-[codex] `service-common` is a shared Java library version, not the deployment version
[codex] --
[codex] 2273-[codex] - clone the repos side by side
[codex] 2274-[codex] - open the sibling `orchestration/` repo
[codex] 2275-[codex] - run `./setup.sh`
[codex] 2276-[codex] - run `tilt up`
[codex] 2277-[codex] 
[codex] 2278:[codex] you should not need `GITHUB_ACTOR`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a personal access token
[codex] 2279-[codex] just to start the app locally.
[codex] 2280-[codex] 
[codex] 2281-[codex] That works because the local build path stays local-first:
[codex] 2282-[codex] 
[codex] 2283:[codex] - Java service repos keep `mavenLocal()` first
[codex] 2284-[codex] - Tilt runs the `service-common-publish` resource first
[codex] 2285-[codex] - `service-common-publish` runs `publishToMavenLocal`
[codex] 2286-[codex] - downstream host-side Gradle builds then resolve `service-common` from Maven
[codex] 2287-[codex]   Local
[codex] 2288-[codex] 
[codex] --
[codex] 2312-[codex]   `currency-service`, `permission-service`, and `session-gateway`
[codex] 2313-[codex] - release-image Docker builds
[codex] 2314-[codex] - isolated CI or clean-builder image builds
[codex] 2315-[codex] - manual clean-shell verification of remote published artifacts
[codex] 2316-[codex] 
[codex] 2317:[codex] `service-common` itself now refreshes the current checked-in snapshot from
[codex] 2318:[codex] `main` through its `publish-snapshot.yml` workflow. That remote snapshot path
[codex] 2319-[codex] exists to support isolated CI/release builds; it does not change the local
[codex] 2320-[codex] contributor contract.
[codex] 2321-[codex] 
[codex] 2322-[codex] For the Java consumer repos, the default build workflow contract is now:
[codex] 2323-[codex] 
[codex] 2324-[codex] - read the pinned `serviceCommon` version from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
[codex] 2325-[codex] - fail fast if `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` or
[codex] 2326-[codex]   `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN` is missing
[codex] 2327-[codex] - preflight the versioned `service-core` and `service-web` POM URLs in GitHub
[codex] 2328-[codex]   Packages
[codex] 2329:[codex] - run `./gradlew build` with `GITHUB_ACTOR` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported from
[codex] 2330-[codex]   that same secret pair
[codex] 2331-[codex]

[codex] 3326-[codex] ## Usage
[codex] 3327-[codex] 
[codex] 3328-[codex] For local development, publish these artifacts to Maven Local and consume them from sibling
[codex] 3329:[codex] Budget Analyzer services. CI workflows publish snapshot and release artifacts to GitHub Packages.
[codex] 3330-[codex] Examples below use `&lt;service-common-version>` as a placeholder. Use the checked-in version literal
[codex] 3331-[codex] from `build.gradle.kts` for the build you are consuming.
[codex] 3332-[codex] 
[codex] 3333-[codex] ### Which Module Should I Use?
[codex] 3334-[codex] 
[codex] --
[codex] 3371-[codex]     // Recommended: Use service-web (includes service-core transitively)
[codex] 3372-[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] 3373-[codex] }
[codex] 3374-[codex] 
[codex] 3375-[codex] repositories {
[codex] 3376:[codex]     mavenLocal()
[codex] 3377-[codex]     mavenCentral()
[codex] 3378-[codex] }
[codex] 3379-[codex] ```
[codex] 3380-[codex] 
[codex] 3381-[codex] The platform artifacts only provide dependency version management. They do not provide beans,
[codex] --
[codex] 3464-[codex] `build.gradle.kts` and is the supported local development path. It does not
[codex] 3465-[codex] require GitHub credentials and does not publish anything remotely. The build
[codex] 3466-[codex] workflow also runs `publishToMavenLocal` as a validation step only.
[codex] 3467-[codex] 
[codex] 3468-[codex] Remote publishing is handled by this repository's workflows:
[codex] 3469:[codex] `publish-snapshot.yml` publishes `-SNAPSHOT` versions from `main` to GitHub
[codex] 3470-[codex] Packages, and `publish-release.yml` publishes tag-driven releases to GitHub
[codex] 3471-[codex] Packages. Successful remote publishes write the published version and GitHub
[codex] 3472-[codex] Packages links to the workflow log and job summary. See
[codex] 3473-[codex] [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md) for
[codex] 3474-[codex] the release and publishing contract.

[codex] 3858:[codex] # The `dependency-submission` action
[codex] 3859-[codex] 
[codex] 3860:[codex] The `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action provides the simplest (and recommended) way to generate a 
[codex] 3861:[codex] dependency graph for your project. This action will attempt to detect all dependencies used by your build
[codex] 3862-[codex] without building and testing the project itself.
[codex] 3863-[codex] 
[codex] 3864:[codex] The dependency graph snapshot is generated via integration with the [GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin](https://plugins.gradle.org/plugin/org.gradle.github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin), and submitted to your repository via the 
[codex] 3865:[codex] [GitHub Dependency Submission API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission).
[codex] 3866:[codex] The generated snapshot files can be submitted in the same job, or saved for submission in a subsequent job.
[codex] 3867-[codex] 
[codex] 3868:[codex] The generated dependency graph includes all of the dependencies in your build, and is used by GitHub to generate 
[codex] 3869-[codex] [Dependabot Alerts](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/dependabot-alerts/about-dependabot-alerts) 
[codex] 3870-[codex] for vulnerable dependencies, as well as to populate the 
[codex] 3871-[codex] [Dependency Graph insights view](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/supply-chain-security/understanding-your-software-supply-chain/exploring-the-dependencies-of-a-repository#viewing-the-dependency-graph).
[codex] 3872-[codex] 
[codex] 3873:[codex] If you're confused by the behaviour you're seeing or have specific questions, please check out [the FAQ](dependency-submission-faq.md) before raising an issue.
[codex] 3874-[codex] 
[codex] 3875-[codex] > [!NOTE]
[codex] 3876-[codex] > ### ⚡️ Choice of caching providers in v6
[codex] 3877-[codex] > To provide the fastest possible build experience this action includes **Enhanced Caching** via `gradle-actions-caching`, an optimized provider powered by proprietary technology. This feature is **free for all public repositories** and is currently available as a **Free Preview** for private repositories. 
[codex] 3878-[codex] >
[codex] --
[codex] 3881-[codex] >
[codex] 3882-[codex] > For a full breakdown of the components, usage tiers, and our **Safe Harbor** data privacy commitment, see our [Distribution & Licensing Guide](../DISTRIBUTION.md).
[codex] 3883-[codex] 
[codex] 3884-[codex] ## General usage
[codex] 3885-[codex] 
[codex] 3886:[codex] The following workflow will generate a dependency graph for a Gradle project and submit it immediately to the repository via the
[codex] 3887-[codex] Dependency Submission API. For most projects, this default configuration should be all that you need.
[codex] 3888-[codex] 
[codex] 3889:[codex] Simply add this as a new workflow file to your repository (eg `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml`).
[codex] 3890-[codex] 
[codex] 3891-[codex] ```yaml
[codex] 3892-[codex] name: Dependency Submission
[codex] 3893-[codex] 
[codex] 3894-[codex] on:
[codex] --
[codex] 3897-[codex] 
[codex] 3898-[codex] permissions:
[codex] 3899-[codex]   contents: write
[codex] 3900-[codex] 
[codex] 3901-[codex] jobs:
[codex] 3902:[codex]   dependency-submission:
[codex] 3903-[codex]     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex] 3904-[codex]     steps:
[codex] 3905-[codex]     - uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] 3906-[codex]     - uses: actions/setup-java@v5
[codex] 3907-[codex]       with:
[codex] 3908-[codex]         distribution: temurin
[codex] 3909-[codex]         java-version: 17
[codex] 3910-[codex] 
[codex] 3911:[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex] 3912:[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 3913-[codex] ```
[codex] 3914-[codex] 
[codex] 3915-[codex] ### Gradle execution
[codex] 3916-[codex] 
[codex] 3917:[codex] To generate a dependency graph, the `dependency-submission` action must perform a Gradle execution that resolves
[codex] 3918-[codex] the dependencies of the project. All dependencies that are resolved in this execution will be included in the 
[codex] 3919:[codex] generated dependency graph.  By default action executes a built-in task that is designed to resolve all build dependencies
[codex] 3920-[codex] (`:ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies`).
[codex] 3921-[codex] 
[codex] 3922-[codex] The action looks for a Gradle project in the root of the workspace, and executes this project with
[codex] 3923-[codex] the Gradle wrapper, if configured for the project. If the wrapper is not configured, whatever `gradle` available
[codex] 3924-[codex] on the command-line will be used.
[codex] --
[codex] 3937-[codex] cache-disabled: true
[codex] 3938-[codex] ```
[codex] 3939-[codex] 
[codex] 3940-[codex] ### Publishing a Develocity Build Scan® from your dependency submission workflow
[codex] 3941-[codex] 
[codex] 3942:[codex] You can automatically publish a free Develocity Build Scan on every run of `gradle/actions/dependency-submission`. 
[codex] 3943-[codex] Three input parameters are required, one to enable publishing and two more to accept the 
[codex] 3944-[codex] [Develocity terms of use](https://gradle.com/help/legal-terms-of-use).
[codex] 3945-[codex] 
[codex] 3946-[codex] ```yaml
[codex] 3947:[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex] 3948:[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 3949-[codex]       with:
[codex] 3950-[codex]         build-scan-publish: true
[codex] 3951-[codex]         build-scan-terms-of-use-url: "https://gradle.com/help/legal-terms-of-use"
[codex] 3952-[codex]         build-scan-terms-of-use-agree: "yes"
[codex] 3953-[codex] ```
[codex] --
[codex] 3957-[codex] ### Configuration parameters
[codex] 3958-[codex] 
[codex] 3959-[codex] In some cases, the default action configuration will not be sufficient, and additional action parameters will need to be specified.
[codex] 3960-[codex] 
[codex] 3961-[codex] ```yaml
[codex] 3962:[codex]     - name: Generate and save dependency graph
[codex] 3963:[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 3964-[codex]       with:
[codex] 3965-[codex]         # Use a particular Gradle version instead of the configured wrapper.
[codex] 3966-[codex]         gradle-version: '8.6'
[codex] 3967-[codex] 
[codex] 3968-[codex]         # The gradle project is not in the root of the repository.
[codex] --
[codex] 3981-[codex]         dependency-graph: generate-and-upload
[codex] 3982-[codex] 
[codex] 3983-[codex]         # Change the number of days that workflow artifacts are retained. (Default is 30 days).
[codex] 3984-[codex]         artifact-retention-days: 5
[codex] 3985-[codex] 
[codex] 3986:[codex]         # Specify the location where dependency graph files will be generated.
[codex] 3987-[codex]         dependency-graph-report-dir: custom-report-dir
[codex] 3988-[codex] 
[codex] 3989:[codex]         # By default, failure to generate a dependency graph will cause the workflow to fail
[codex] 3990-[codex]         dependency-graph-continue-on-failure: true
[codex] 3991-[codex] 
[codex] 3992-[codex] ```
[codex] 3993-[codex] 
[codex] 3994:[codex] See the [Action Metadata file](../dependency-submission/action.yml) for a more detailed description of each input parameter.
[codex] 3995-[codex] 
[codex] 3996-[codex] The `GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin` can be further 
[codex] 3997-[codex] [configured via a number of environment variables](https://github.com/gradle/github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin?#required-environment-variables). 
[codex] 3998:[codex] These will be automatically set by the `dependency-submission` action, but you may override these values 
[codex] 3999-[codex] by setting them explicitly in your workflow file.
[codex] 4000-[codex] 
[codex] 4001:[codex] ### Reducing storage costs for saved dependency graph artifacts
[codex] 4002-[codex] 
[codex] 4003:[codex] By default, the dependency graph that is generated is stored as a workflow artifact.
[codex] 4004-[codex] To reduce storage costs for these artifacts, you can:
[codex] 4005-[codex] 
[codex] 4006-[codex] 1. Set the `artifact-retention-days`:
[codex] 4007-[codex] 
[codex] 4008-[codex] ```yaml
[codex] 4009:[codex]     - name: Generate dependency graph but only store workflow artifacts for 1 day
[codex] 4010:[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 4011-[codex]       with:
[codex] 4012-[codex]         artifact-retention-days: 1 # Default is 30 days or as configured for repository
[codex] 4013-[codex] ```
[codex] 4014-[codex] 
[codex] 4015-[codex] 2. Disable storing dependency-graph artifacts using `generate-and-submit`
[codex] 4016-[codex] 
[codex] 4017-[codex] ```yaml
[codex] 4018:[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph but do not store as workflow artifact
[codex] 4019:[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 4020-[codex]       with:
[codex] 4021-[codex]         dependency-graph: 'generate-and-submit' # Default value is 'generate-submit-and-upload'
[codex] 4022-[codex] ```
[codex] 4023-[codex] 
[codex] 4024-[codex] # Resolving a dependency vulnerability
[codex] 4025-[codex] 
[codex] 4026-[codex] ## Finding the source of a dependency vulnerability
[codex] 4027-[codex] 
[codex] 4028:[codex] Once you have submitted a dependency graph, you may receive Dependabot Alerts warning about vulnerabilities in
[codex] 4029-[codex] dependencies of your project. In the case of transitive dependencies, it may not be obvious how that dependency is
[codex] 4030-[codex] used or what you can do to address the vulnerability alert.
[codex] 4031-[codex] 
[codex] 4032-[codex] The first step to investigating a Dependabot Alert is to determine the source of the dependency. One of the best ways to 
[codex] 4033-[codex] do so is with a free Develocity Build Scan®, which makes it easy to explore the dependencies resolved in your build.
[codex] --
[codex] 4042-[codex] offending dependency.
[codex] 4043-[codex] 
[codex] 4044-[codex] ### When you cannot publish a Build Scan®
[codex] 4045-[codex] 
[codex] 4046-[codex] If publishing a free Build Scan to https://scans.gradle.com isn't an option, and you don't have access to a private [Develocity
[codex] 4047:[codex] server](https://gradle.com/) for your project, you can obtain information about the each resolved dependency by running the `dependency-submission` workflow with debug logging enabled.
[codex] 4048-[codex] 
[codex] 4049:[codex] The simplest way to do so is to re-run the dependency-submission job with debug logging enabled:
[codex] 4050-[codex] 
[codex] 4051-[codex] &lt;img width="665" alt="image" src="https://github.com/gradle/actions/assets/179734/d95b889a-09fb-4731-91f2-baebbf647e31">
[codex] 4052-[codex] 
[codex] 4053-[codex] When you do so, the Gradle build that generates the dependency-graph will include a log message for each dependency version included in the graph.
[codex] 4054-[codex] Given the details in one log message, you can run (locally) the built-in [dependencyInsight](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/viewing_debugging_dependencies.html#dependency_insights) task
[codex] --
[codex] 4128-[codex] plugins {
[codex] 4129-[codex]   id("com.github.ben-manes.versions") version("0.51.0")
[codex] 4130-[codex] }
[codex] 4131-[codex] ```
[codex] 4132-[codex] 
[codex] 4133:[codex] ## Limiting the dependencies that appear in the dependency graph
[codex] 4134-[codex] 
[codex] 4135:[codex] By default, the `dependency-submission` action attempts to detect all dependencies declared and used by your Gradle build.
[codex] 4136-[codex] At times it may helpful to limit the dependencies reported to GitHub, to avoid security alerts for dependencies that 
[codex] 4137-[codex] don't form a critical part of your product. For example, a vulnerability in the tool you use to generate documentation 
[codex] 4138-[codex] may not be as important as a vulnerability in one of your runtime dependencies.
[codex] 4139-[codex] 
[codex] 4140:[codex] The `dependency-submission` action provides a convenient mechanism to filter the projects and configurations that
[codex] 4141:[codex] contribute to the dependency graph.
[codex] 4142-[codex] 
[codex] 4143-[codex] > [!NOTE]
[codex] 4144:[codex] > Ideally, all dependencies involved in building and testing a project will be extracted and reported in a dependency graph. 
[codex] 4145-[codex] > These dependencies would be assigned to different scopes (eg development, runtime, testing) and the GitHub UI would make it easy to opt-in to security alerts for different dependency scopes.
[codex] 4146-[codex] > However, this functionality does not yet exist.
[codex] 4147-[codex] 
[codex] 4148:[codex] ### Selecting Gradle projects that will contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] 4149-[codex] 
[codex] 4150:[codex] If you do not want the dependency graph to include dependencies from every project in your build, 
[codex] 4151-[codex] you can easily exclude or include certain projects from the dependency extraction process.
[codex] 4152-[codex] 
[codex] 4153-[codex] To restrict which Gradle subprojects contribute to the report, specify which projects to exclude or include via a regular expression.
[codex] 4154-[codex] You can use the `dependency-graph-exclude-projects` and `dependency-graph-include-projects` input parameters for this purpose.
[codex] 4155-[codex] 
[codex] 4156-[codex] Note that excluding a project in this way only removes dependencies that are _resolved_ as part of that project, and may
[codex] 4157-[codex] not necessarily remove all dependencies _declared_ in that project. If another project depends on the excluded project
[codex] 4158-[codex] then it may transitively resolve dependencies declared in the excluded project: these dependencies will still be included
[codex] 4159:[codex] in the generated dependency graph.
[codex] 4160-[codex] 
[codex] 4161:[codex] ### Selecting Gradle configurations that will contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] 4162-[codex] 
[codex] 4163:[codex] Similarly to Gradle projects, it is possible to exclude or include a set of dependency configurations from dependency graph generation,
[codex] 4164-[codex] so that only dependencies resolved by the included configurations are reported.
[codex] 4165-[codex] 
[codex] 4166-[codex] To restrict which Gradle configurations contribute to the report, specify which configurations to exclude or include via a regular expression.
[codex] 4167-[codex] You can use the `dependency-graph-exclude-configurations` and `dependency-graph-include-configurations` input parameters for this purpose.
[codex] 4168-[codex] 
[codex] --
[codex] 4173-[codex] ### Example of project and configuration filtering
[codex] 4174-[codex] 
[codex] 4175-[codex] For example, if you want to exclude dependencies resolved by the `buildSrc` project, and exclude dependencies from the `testCompileClasspath` and `testRuntimeClasspath` configurations, you would use the following configuration:
[codex] 4176-[codex] 
[codex] 4177-[codex] ```yaml
[codex] 4178:[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex] 4179:[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] 4180-[codex]       with:
[codex] 4181-[codex]         # Exclude all dependencies that originate solely in the 'buildSrc' project
[codex] 4182-[codex]         dependency-graph-exclude-projects: ':buildSrc'
[codex] 4183-[codex]         # Exclude dependencies that are only resolved in test classpaths
[codex] 4184-[codex]         dependency-graph-exclude-configurations: '.*[Tt]est(Compile|Runtime)Classpath'
[codex] --
[codex] 4194-[codex] 
[codex] 4195-[codex] ## Integrating the `dependency-review-action`
[codex] 4196-[codex] 
[codex] 4197-[codex] The GitHub [dependency-review-action](https://github.com/actions/dependency-review-action) helps you 
[codex] 4198-[codex] understand dependency changes (and the security impact of these changes) for a pull request,
[codex] 4199:[codex] by comparing the dependency graph for the pull-request with that of the HEAD commit.
[codex] 4200-[codex] 
[codex] 4201-[codex] Integrating the Dependency Review Action requires 2 changes to your workflows:
[codex] 4202-[codex] 
[codex] 4203-[codex] #### 1. Add a `pull_request` trigger to your existing Dependency Submission workflow.
[codex] 4204-[codex] 
[codex] 4205:[codex] In order to perform Dependency Review on a pull request, the dependency graph must be submitted for the pull request.
[codex] 4206-[codex] To do this, simply add a `pull_request` trigger to your existing dependency submission workflow.
[codex] 4207-[codex] 
[codex] 4208-[codex] ```yaml
[codex] 4209-[codex] name: Dependency Submission
[codex] 4210-[codex] 
[codex] --
[codex] 4214-[codex]   pull_request:
[codex] 4215-[codex] 
[codex] 4216-[codex] permissions:
[codex] 4217-[codex]   contents: write
[codex] 4218-[codex] name: Gradle Dependency Submission
[codex] 4219:[codex] description: Generates a dependency graph for a Gradle project and submits it via the Dependency Submission API
[codex] 4220-[codex] 
[codex] 4221-[codex] inputs:
[codex] 4222-[codex]   # Gradle execution configuration
[codex] 4223-[codex]   gradle-version:
[codex] 4224-[codex]     description: |
[codex] --
[codex] 4236-[codex]       By default, the built-in `:ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies` task is executed.
[codex] 4237-[codex]     required: false
[codex] 4238-[codex] 
[codex] 4239-[codex]   additional-arguments:
[codex] 4240-[codex]     description: |
[codex] 4241:[codex]       Additional arguments to pass to Gradle when generating the dependency graph.
[codex] 4242-[codex]       For example, `--no-configuration-cache --stacktrace`.
[codex] 4243-[codex]     required: false
[codex] 4244-[codex] 
[codex] 4245-[codex]   # Cache configuration
[codex] 4246-[codex]   cache-provider:
[codex] --
[codex] 4321-[codex] 
[codex] 4322-[codex]   # Dependency Graph configuration
[codex] 4323-[codex]   dependency-graph:
[codex] 4324-[codex]     description: |
[codex] 4325-[codex]       Specifies how the dependency-graph should be handled by this action. 
[codex] 4326:[codex]       By default a dependency-graph will be generated, submitted to the dependency-submission API, and saved as a workflow artifact.
[codex] 4327-[codex]       Valid values are:
[codex] 4328:[codex]         'generate-and-submit': Generates a dependency graph for the project and submits it in the same Job.
[codex] 4329:[codex]         'generate-submit-and-upload (default)': As per 'generate-and-submit', but also saves the dependency graph as a workflow artifact.
[codex] 4330:[codex]         'generate-and-upload': Generates a dependency graph for the project and saves it as a workflow artifact. Does not submit it to the repository.
[codex] 4331-[codex]         'download-and-submit': Retrieves a previously saved dependency-graph and submits it to the repository.
[codex] 4332-[codex] 
[codex] 4333-[codex]       Use `generate-and-submit` if you prefer not to save the dependency-graph as a workflow artifact.
[codex] 4334-[codex]       The `generate-and-upload` and `download-and-submit` options are designed to be used in an untrusted workflow scenario,
[codex] 4335-[codex]       where the workflow generating the dependency-graph cannot (or should not) be given the `contents: write` permissions
[codex] --
[codex] 4337-[codex]     required: false
[codex] 4338-[codex]     default: 'generate-submit-and-upload'
[codex] 4339-[codex] 
[codex] 4340-[codex]   dependency-graph-report-dir:
[codex] 4341-[codex]     description: |
[codex] 4342:[codex]       Specifies where the dependency graph report will be generated. 
[codex] 4343-[codex]       Paths can relative or absolute. Relative paths are resolved relative to the workspace directory.
[codex] 4344-[codex]     required: false
[codex] 4345-[codex]     default: 'dependency-graph-reports'
[codex] 4346-[codex] 
[codex] 4347-[codex]   dependency-graph-continue-on-failure:
[codex] 4348:[codex]     description: When 'false' a failure to generate or submit a dependency graph will fail the Step or Job. When 'true' a warning will be emitted but no failure will result.
[codex] 4349-[codex]     required: false
[codex] 4350-[codex]     default: false
[codex] 4351-[codex] 
[codex] 4352-[codex]   dependency-graph-exclude-projects:
[codex] 4353-[codex]     description: |
[codex] 4354:[codex]       Gradle projects that should be excluded from dependency graph (regular expression).
[codex] 4355-[codex]       When set, any matching project will be excluded.
[codex] 4356-[codex]     required: false
[codex] 4357-[codex] 
[codex] 4358-[codex]   dependency-graph-include-projects:
[codex] 4359-[codex]     description: |
[codex] 4360:[codex]       Gradle projects that should be included in dependency graph (regular expression). 
[codex] 4361-[codex]       When set, only matching projects will be included.
[codex] 4362-[codex]     required: false
[codex] 4363-[codex] 
[codex] 4364-[codex]   dependency-graph-exclude-configurations:
[codex] 4365-[codex]     description: |
[codex] 4366:[codex]       Gradle configurations that should be included in dependency graph (regular expression). 
[codex] 4367-[codex]       When set, anymatching configurations will be excluded.

[codex] [codex] - HTTP request/response logging filters
[codex] [codex] - Correlation ID support

[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Dependencies**: service-core (transitive), Spring Boot Starter Web/WebFlux, SpringDoc OpenAPI
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Note**: Actuator (health checks, metrics) comes from service-core and is available to all services (web and non-web).

[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Purpose
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Service Common promotes code reuse and consistency across microservices by:
[codex] [codex] - Reducing code duplication
[codex] [codex] - Ensuring consistent data models
[codex] [codex] - Providing shared utilities
[codex] [codex] - Standardizing error handling and validation
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Technology Stack
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - **Java 25**
[codex] [codex] - **Spring Boot 3.x** (Starter Web, Data JPA)
[codex] [codex] - **SpringDoc OpenAPI** for API documentation
[codex] [codex] - **OpenCSV** for CSV file processing
[codex] [codex] - **JUnit 5** for testing
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Usage
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For local development, publish these artifacts to Maven Local and consume them from sibling
[codex] [codex] Budget Analyzer services. CI workflows publish snapshot and release artifacts to GitHub Packages.
[codex] [codex] Examples below use `&lt;service-common-version>` as a placeholder. Use the checked-in version literal
[codex] [codex] from `build.gradle.kts` for the build you are consuming.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Which Module Should I Use?
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Most microservices should use `service-web`** (which transitively includes `service-core`):
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```kotlin
[codex] [codex] dependencies {
[codex] [codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex] [codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Spring Cloud services should use `spring-cloud-platform`** instead of importing both platforms:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```kotlin
[codex] [codex] dependencies {
[codex] [codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-cloud-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex] [codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Use `service-core` alone** only if you need minimal dependencies without Spring Web features:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```kotlin
[codex] [codex] dependencies {
[codex] [codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex] [codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-core:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Adding as a Dependency
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] In your service's `build.gradle.kts`:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```kotlin
[codex] [codex] dependencies {
[codex] [codex]     // Import dependency versions explicitly.
[codex] [codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]     // Recommended: Use service-web (includes service-core transitively)
[codex] [codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] repositories {
[codex] [codex]     mavenLocal()
[codex] [codex]     mavenCentral()
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The platform artifacts only provide dependency version management. They do not provide beans,
[codex] [codex] filters, exception handlers, or autoconfiguration metadata. Runtime behavior comes from
[codex] [codex] `service-core` and `service-web`.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### What You Get Automatically
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The runtime modules use **Spring Boot autoconfiguration** - all components are automatically registered when the libraries are on your classpath. **No manual configuration or component scanning required!**
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### service-core (Fully Automatic)
[codex] [codex] When service-core is on your classpath, you automatically get:
[codex] [codex] - **JPA Entity Scanning** - Base entities (AuditableEntity, SoftDeletableEntity) automatically discovered
[codex] [codex] - **JPA Auditing** - Automatic timestamps (createdAt, updatedAt) on entities
[codex] [codex] - **CSV Parser Bean** - OpenCsvParser available for injection
[codex] [codex] - **Prometheus Metrics** - `/actuator/prometheus` endpoint exposed automatically for scraping
[codex] [codex] - **`application` Common Tag** - Every Micrometer meter is tagged with `application=${spring.application.name}` so metrics carry service identity independent of the scrape pipeline (override with `management.metrics.tags.application`)
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### service-web (Mostly Automatic)
[codex] [codex] When service-web is on your classpath, you automatically get:
[codex] [codex] - **Global Exception Handler** - All exceptions converted to standardized ApiErrorResponse format
[codex] [codex]   - Maps: 400 (Bad Request), 404 (Not Found), 422 (Business Logic), 500 (Server Error)
[codex] [codex] - **Claims Header Security** - Automatic stateless authentication from trusted ingress external-auth headers
[codex] [codex]   - Reads `X-User-Id`, `X-Permissions`, `X-Roles` headers for both servlet and reactive services
[codex] [codex]   - Public endpoints: health, prometheus, and OpenAPI routes; all other endpoints, including `/internal/**`, require service-owned rules or authenticated claims headers
[codex] [codex] - **Correlation ID Filter** - Automatically adds correlation IDs to all requests and regenerates malformed inbound values before they reach logs or response headers
[codex] [codex] - **HTTP Logging Filter** - Optional (enable with `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.enabled=true`)
[codex] [codex]   - Text bodies only: common secret fields in JSON and form payloads are redacted
[codex] [codex]   - Multipart, binary, and compressed bodies are omitted with placeholders instead of raw content
[codex] [codex]   - Required list settings and `log-level` must not be explicit null values; startup fails during
[codex] [codex]     configuration validation if they are bound as null. Empty lists are valid.
[codex] [codex] - **OpenAPI Base Config** - Standard error response schemas (extend BaseOpenApiConfig in your service)
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Example configuration** (application.yml):
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex] budgetanalyzer:
[codex] [codex]   service:
[codex] [codex]     http-logging:
[codex] [codex]       enabled: true
[codex] [codex]       include-query-params: false # Optional: defaults to false
[codex] [codex]       include-request-body: true   # Optional: defaults to false; JSON/form secrets are redacted
[codex] [codex]       include-response-body: true  # Optional: defaults to false; binary/multipart/compressed bodies are omitted
[codex] [codex]       max-body-size: 10000         # Optional: values &lt;= 0 disable body capture
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **No component scanning needed** - Spring Boot autoconfiguration handles everything via `META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports`.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The shared claims-header security configuration provides the default Spring Security filter chain.
[codex] [codex] If a consuming service defines its own `SecurityFilterChain` or `SecurityWebFilterChain`, the
[codex] [codex] shared default chain backs off so the service's custom rules win deterministically.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### Discovering What's Autoconfigured
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```bash
[codex] [codex] # View autoconfiguration registration files
[codex] [codex] cat service-core/src/main/resources/META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports
[codex] [codex] cat service-web/src/main/resources/META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # View autoconfiguration classes
[codex] [codex] cat service-core/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/core/config/ServiceCoreAutoConfiguration.java
[codex] [codex] cat service-web/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/service/config/ServiceWebAutoConfiguration.java
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # View global exception handler
[codex] [codex] cat service-web/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/service/servlet/api/ServletApiExceptionHandler.java
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # View security configuration
[codex] [codex] cat service-web/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/service/security/ClaimsHeaderSecurityConfig.java
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Find all @Configuration classes
[codex] [codex] grep -r "@Configuration\|@AutoConfiguration" service-*/src/main/java/
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For complete details, see the [Autoconfiguration section in AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#autoconfiguration).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Building and Publishing
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```bash
[codex] [codex] # Build all modules
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Publish all service-common artifacts to Maven Local for local development
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew publishToMavenLocal
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] `publishToMavenLocal` uses the checked-in version literal from
[codex] [codex] `build.gradle.kts` and is the supported local development path. It does not
[codex] [codex] require GitHub credentials and does not publish anything remotely. The build
[codex] [codex] workflow also runs `publishToMavenLocal` as a validation step only.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Remote publishing is handled by this repository's workflows:
[codex] [codex] `publish-snapshot.yml` publishes `-SNAPSHOT` versions from `main` to GitHub
[codex] [codex] Packages, and `publish-release.yml` publishes tag-driven releases to GitHub
[codex] [codex] Packages. Successful remote publishes write the published version and GitHub
[codex] [codex] Packages links to the workflow log and job summary. See
[codex] [codex] [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md) for
[codex] [codex] the release and publishing contract.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] `service-common`'s own version and backwards-compatibility contract lives in
[codex] [codex] [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md).
[codex] [codex]

[codex] [codex] ### Building
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```bash
[codex] [codex] # Clean and build
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Run tests
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew test
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Run tests and generate JaCoCo coverage reports
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Check code style
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew spotlessCheck
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Apply code formatting
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] JaCoCo report generation is configured for the Java library modules only:
[codex] [codex] `service-core/build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html` and
[codex] [codex] `service-web/build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html`. XML reports are written
[codex] [codex] next to the HTML reports for CI/dashboard ingestion. `check` enforces the
[codex] [codex] configured coverage gates.

[codex] [codex] ## Code Quality
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] This project enforces code quality through:
[codex] [codex] - **Google Java Format** for consistent code style
[codex] [codex] - **Checkstyle** for code standards
[codex] [codex] - **Spotless** for automated formatting
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Conventions
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Prefixed String IDs
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] All cross-service identifiers use the format `{prefix}_{full-uuid-hex}` (e.g., `usr_507f1f77bcf86cd799439011abcdef12`, `req_a1b2c3d4e5f6a7b8c9d0e1f2a3b4c5d6`).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - **Why strings over longs**: These IDs flow across service boundaries and into JWTs. Auto-increment longs couple identity to a single database; string UUIDs are portable by default.
[codex] [codex] - **Why prefixes**: `usr_`, `txn_`, `req_` make IDs self-describing in logs, JWTs, and database queries.
[codex] [codex] - **Why full UUIDs**: Always use 32 hex chars (128 bits). Never truncate — entropy reduction with no storage benefit since the type is `String` either way.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] See the [Vendor Independence section in AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#vendor-independence) for the full rationale.
[codex] [codex]
